People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Well, um… I guess the main thing is my talking has been getting kind of mixed up. Not all the time, but enough that people tell me they can’t follow me.

I’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I thought maybe I should come in and talk to someone, if that’s okay.

I’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I thought maybe I should come in and talk to someone, if that’s okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I do feel kind of up. Not bad, exactly—like I’ve got a lot in me, like I can talk to anybody and I’ll laugh loud and feel… strong, I guess. Powerful maybe, but I don’t mean that in a weird way.\n\nAnd, um… if somebody questions me when I’m like that, I can get irritated fast. Like I might snap or talk over them, even shout, over something small. I’m not proud of that. It just comes out before I can settle myself. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you’re feeling especially up like that, do you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but when I’m like that I can sleep maybe two, three hours and then I’m up before dawn feeling… fine. Fresh, even.\n\nLike I’ll start making plans for work or what needs cleaning, like sleep is something other people need and I don’t really have to bother with it. That’s probably not great, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic or anything, but I have heard… like a man’s voice.\n\nIt’s usually when I’m at work, or around the building—like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet. It’ll say my name, or say I ruined everything. And it sounds real, like somebody’s standing right beside me.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound good. I just… I’m trying to answer you right.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that voice during times when you were not feeling especially up or energized?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. Not every time, but there were calmer weeks where I wasn’t really all up and full of energy, and I wasn’t… you know, way down either.\n\nI still heard him then. The man’s voice, like in the vents. And I still had that feeling—well, more than a feeling—that people in the building were signaling things about me. It went on for more than a couple weeks, I guess.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was energized.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I just gave up or anything, but there have been stretches where most days I’d wake up and just feel… heavy.\n\nLike after morning prayer, I’d sit on the edge of the bed and just stay there a while. Empty, I guess. Like there wasn’t much point in cleaning myself up or going outside.\n\nIt’s hard to say it the right way, but yes… it has lasted longer than two weeks before.\n\nDoctor: Across the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and people signaling about you, have you been in either a clear manic state or a depressed state for most of that time?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I think most of the time it’s been when I’m more keyed up.\n\nI don’t mean out of control, exactly, but I get going and I talk too much, and I start making these big cleaning plans that are probably more than I can really do. It feels like I have to keep moving, like I can’t slow myself down.\n\nSo… yes, if I’m answering it right, I’d say most of the time since all this started, I’ve been in those stretches more than the low ones.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms begin after you had a medical illness, head injury, seizure, or started using any drugs or new medicines?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… not that I can point to.\n\nI mean, I haven’t had some big head injury or anything like that that I remember. And I’m not aware of seizures. As far as drugs or new medicines, I don’t think it started from anything like that.\n\nIf there’s a certain kind of illness or medicine you mean, you can ask me straight, but… I don’t think that’s how it began.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling people were signaling about you while you were not manic and not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there were times like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were some calmer weeks where I wasn’t really up like that, and I wasn’t down in that heavy way either. I was just kind of… normal enough, I guess.\n\nBut I still heard the man in the vents during that time. And I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. I just… I thought maybe I should come in and talk to someone, if that’s okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I do feel kind of up. Not bad, exactly—like I’ve got a lot in me, like I can talk to anybody and I’ll laugh loud and feel… strong, I guess. Powerful maybe, but I don’t mean that in a weird way.\n\nAnd, um… if somebody questions me when I’m like that, I can get irritated fast. Like I might snap or talk over them, even shout, over something small. I’m not proud of that. It just comes out before I can settle myself. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you’re feeling especially up like that, do you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but when I’m like that I can sleep maybe two, three hours and then I’m up before dawn feeling… fine. Fresh, even.\n\nLike I’ll start making plans for work or what needs cleaning, like sleep is something other people need and I don’t really have to bother with it. That’s probably not great, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic or anything, but I have heard… like a man’s voice.\n\nIt’s usually when I’m at work, or around the building—like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet. It’ll say my name, or say I ruined everything. And it sounds real, like somebody’s standing right beside me.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound good. I just… I’m trying to answer you right.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that voice during times when you were not feeling especially up or energized?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. Not every time, but there were calmer weeks where I wasn’t really all up and full of energy, and I wasn’t… you know, way down either.\n\nI still heard him then. The man’s voice, like in the vents. And I still had that feeling—well, more than a feeling—that people in the building were signaling things about me. It went on for more than a couple weeks, I guess.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was energized.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I just gave up or anything, but there have been stretches where most days I’d wake up and just feel… heavy.\n\nLike after morning prayer, I’d sit on the edge of the bed and just stay there a while. Empty, I guess. Like there wasn’t much point in cleaning myself up or going outside.\n\nIt’s hard to say it the right way, but yes… it has lasted longer than two weeks before.\n\nDoctor: Across the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and people signaling about you, have you been in either a clear manic state or a depressed state for most of that time?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I think most of the time it’s been when I’m more keyed up.\n\nI don’t mean out of control, exactly, but I get going and I talk too much, and I start making these big cleaning plans that are probably more than I can really do. It feels like I have to keep moving, like I can’t slow myself down.\n\nSo… yes, if I’m answering it right, I’d say most of the time since all this started, I’ve been in those stretches more than the low ones.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms begin after you had a medical illness, head injury, seizure, or started using any drugs or new medicines?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… not that I can point to.\n\nI mean, I haven’t had some big head injury or anything like that that I remember. And I’m not aware of seizures. As far as drugs or new medicines, I don’t think it started from anything like that.\n\nIf there’s a certain kind of illness or medicine you mean, you can ask me straight, but… I don’t think that’s how it began.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling people were signaling about you while you were not manic and not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there were times like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were some calmer weeks where I wasn’t really up like that, and I wasn’t down in that heavy way either. I was just kind of… normal enough, I guess.\n\nBut I still heard the man in the vents during that time. And I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. That went on more than two weeks, I’m pretty sure.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and feeling that people were signaling about you come on very suddenly, within about two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor.\n\nIt didn’t feel like one day I was fine and then all of a sudden everything started within a week or two. But I also wasn’t keeping track that carefully, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nI guess it felt more like it kind of built up, but… if there’s a specific way you want me to think about it, I can try.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I thought maybe I should come in and talk to someone, if that’s okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I do feel kind of up. Not bad, exactly—like I’ve got a lot in me, like I can talk to anybody and I’ll laugh loud and feel… strong, I guess. Powerful maybe, but I don’t mean that in a weird way.\n\nAnd, um… if somebody questions me when I’m like that, I can get irritated fast. Like I might snap or talk over them, even shout, over something small. I’m not proud of that. It just comes out before I can settle myself. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but when I’m like that I can sleep maybe two, three hours and then I’m up before dawn feeling… fine. Fresh, even.\n\nLike I’ll start making plans for work or what needs cleaning, like sleep is something other people need and I don’t really have to bother with it. I just… I’m trying to answer you right.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that voice during times when you were not feeling especially up or energized?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. I was just kind of… normal enough, I guess.\n\nBut I still heard the man in the vents during that time. And I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. But I also wasn’t keeping track that carefully, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nI guess it felt more like it kind of built up, but… if there’s a specific way you want me to think about it, I can try.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling that people were signaling about you lasted for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s been more than a month.\n\nI don’t like saying it like that, but the voices and that feeling like people were watching me or marking me somehow… that’s been around for maybe a year and a half now.\n\nSome days it’s not as bad, so I try not to make a big thing of it, but it hasn’t really gone away.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I guess I have, a lot more than I’d like to admit.

I used to do more with people. I liked taking pictures, playing chess, going to the mosque, even cooking extra food for neighbors sometimes. I wasn’t some social butterfly, but I had things I cared about.

Now… the camera just stays in the drawer. And when people call, I don’t always answer. I tell myself I’m just tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel like being around anybody much.

I don’t mean to sound bad. It’s just… yeah, I’m less interested than I used to be.

Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m bragging or anything, but there have been times where I felt really high, like… powerful, like I could handle anything.

But if somebody questioned me or acted like I was wrong, I could snap. Like shout over something small. I’m not proud of that. But yes… I think there have been times like that, at least a week. Is that what you mean?

Not every time, but there were calmer weeks where I wasn’t really all up and full of energy, and I wasn’t… you know, way down either.\n\nI still heard him then. The man’s voice, like in the vents. And I still had that feeling—well, more than a feeling—that people in the building were signaling things about me. It went on for more than a couple weeks, I guess.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was energized.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I just gave up or anything, but there have been stretches where most days I’d wake up and just feel… heavy.\n\nLike after morning prayer, I’d sit on the edge of the bed and just stay there a while. Empty, I guess. Like there wasn’t much point in cleaning myself up or going outside.\n\nIt’s hard to say it the right way, but yes… it has lasted longer than two weeks before.\n\nDoctor: Across the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and people signaling about you, have you been in either a clear manic state or a depressed state for most of that time?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I think most of the time it’s been when I’m more keyed up.\n\nI don’t mean out of control, exactly, but I get going and I talk too much, and I start making these big cleaning plans that are probably more than I can really do. It feels like I have to keep moving, like I can’t slow myself down.\n\nSo… yes, if I’m answering it right, I’d say most of the time since all this started, I’ve been in those stretches more than the low ones.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms begin after you had a medical illness, head injury, seizure, or started using any drugs or new medicines?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… not that I can point to.\n\nI mean, I haven’t had some big head injury or anything like that that I remember. And I’m not aware of seizures. As far as drugs or new medicines, I don’t think it started from anything like that.\n\nIf there’s a certain kind of illness or medicine you mean, you can ask me straight, but… I don’t think that’s how it began.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling people were signaling about you while you were not manic and not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there were times like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were some calmer weeks where I wasn’t really up like that, and I wasn’t down in that heavy way either. I was just kind of… normal enough, I guess.\n\nBut I still heard the man in the vents during that time. And I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. That went on more than two weeks, I’m pretty sure.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and feeling that people were signaling about you come on very suddenly, within about two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor.\n\nIt didn’t feel like one day I was fine and then all of a sudden everything started within a week or two. But I also wasn’t keeping track that carefully, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nI guess it felt more like it kind of built up, but… if there’s a specific way you want me to think about it, I can try.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling that people were signaling about you lasted for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s been more than a month.\n\nI don’t like saying it like that, but the voices and that feeling like people were watching me or marking me somehow… that’s been around for maybe a year and a half now.\n\nSome days it’s not as bad, so I try not to make a big thing of it, but it hasn’t really gone away. Is that the kind of time frame you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, a lot more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI used to do more with people. I tell myself I’m just tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel like being around anybody much.\n\nI don’t mean to sound bad. It’s just… yeah, I’m less interested than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not feeling especially up or especially depressed, do you still find that your interest in people and activities is much lower than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not really high or really down, it’s still there.\n\nI mean, I try to tell myself I’m just getting older or tired from work, but it’s different than before. Yeah… I mean, I still try to manage, doctor. I don’t want it to sound like I just gave up.

I was just kind of… normal enough, I guess.\n\nBut I still heard the man in the vents during that time. And I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. That went on more than two weeks, I’m pretty sure.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and feeling that people were signaling about you come on very suddenly, within about two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor.\n\nIt didn’t feel like one day I was fine and then all of a sudden everything started within a week or two. But I also wasn’t keeping track that carefully, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nI guess it felt more like it kind of built up, but… if there’s a specific way you want me to think about it, I can try.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling that people were signaling about you lasted for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s been more than a month.\n\nI don’t like saying it like that, but the voices and that feeling like people were watching me or marking me somehow… that’s been around for maybe a year and a half now.\n\nSome days it’s not as bad, so I try not to make a big thing of it, but it hasn’t really gone away. Is that the kind of time frame you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, a lot more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI used to do more with people. I liked taking pictures, playing chess, going to the mosque, even cooking extra food for neighbors sometimes. I wasn’t some social butterfly, but I had things I cared about.\n\nNow… the camera just stays in the drawer. And when people call, I don’t always answer. I tell myself I’m just tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel like being around anybody much.\n\nI don’t mean to sound bad. It’s just… yeah, I’m less interested than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not feeling especially up or especially depressed, do you still find that your interest in people and activities is much lower than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not really high or really down, it’s still there.\n\nI mean, I try to tell myself I’m just getting older or tired from work, but it’s different than before. I don’t want to seem rude or ungrateful. I just… don’t feel the pull to do those things like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems overall—the voices, the feeling people were signaling about you, and the changes in your thinking—been going on for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s been more than six months.\n\nI guess if I’m being honest, it started after that brief detention, you know, the misunderstanding at the building. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least one week when you felt unusually energized or powerful most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I’d be laughing a lot, talking loud with everybody, feeling like people should listen to me. And usually I’d think I was just in a good mood, you know?\n\nBut if somebody questioned me or acted like I was wrong, I could snap. Like shout over something small. I’m not proud of that. But yes… I think there have been times like that, at least a week. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Because of these problems, have you become unable to keep up with your work or basic self-care compared with how you were before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still try to manage, doctor. I don’t want it to sound like I just gave up.\n\nBut compared to before, yes. I’ve missed shifts, and I’ve lost some cleaning work because people said they couldn’t rely on me, or they didn’t understand what I was saying sometimes. That was hard to hear.\n\nAnd outside work, I stopped going to the mosque as much, and I don’t really go to family gatherings like I used to. People invite me, but… I don’t always show up, or I leave things messy and unfinished.\n\nThere have been a couple times where it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help. I’m not proud of that. I’m just trying to answer you honestly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring one of those high-energy periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 53 / janitor_or_building_cleaner / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Not every time, but there were calmer weeks where I wasn’t really all up and full of energy, and I wasn’t… you know, way down either.\n\nI still heard him then. The man’s voice, like in the vents. And I still had that feeling—well, more than a feeling—that people in the building were signaling things about me. It went on for more than a couple weeks, I guess.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was energized.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I just gave up or anything, but there have been stretches where most days I’d wake up and just feel… heavy.\n\nLike after morning prayer, I’d sit on the edge of the bed and just stay there a while. Empty, I guess. Like there wasn’t much point in cleaning myself up or going outside.\n\nIt’s hard to say it the right way, but yes… it has lasted longer than two weeks before.\n\nDoctor: Across the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and people signaling about you, have you been in either a clear manic state or a depressed state for most of that time?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I think most of the time it’s been when I’m more keyed up.\n\nI don’t mean out of control, exactly, but I get going and I talk too much, and I start making these big cleaning plans that are probably more than I can really do. It feels like I have to keep moving, like I can’t slow myself down.\n\nSo… yes, if I’m answering it right, I’d say most of the time since all this started, I’ve been in those stretches more than the low ones.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms begin after you had a medical illness, head injury, seizure, or started using any drugs or new medicines?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… not that I can point to.\n\nI mean, I haven’t had some big head injury or anything like that that I remember. And I’m not aware of seizures. As far as drugs or new medicines, I don’t think it started from anything like that.\n\nIf there’s a certain kind of illness or medicine you mean, you can ask me straight, but… I don’t think that’s how it began.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling people were signaling about you while you were not manic and not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there were times like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were some calmer weeks where I wasn’t really up like that, and I wasn’t down in that heavy way either. I was just kind of… normal enough, I guess.\n\nBut I still heard the man in the vents during that time. And I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. That went on more than two weeks, I’m pretty sure.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and feeling that people were signaling about you come on very suddenly, within about two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor.\n\nIt didn’t feel like one day I was fine and then all of a sudden everything started within a week or two. But I also wasn’t keeping track that carefully, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nI guess it felt more like it kind of built up, but… if there’s a specific way you want me to think about it, I can try.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling that people were signaling about you lasted for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s been more than a month.\n\nI don’t like saying it like that, but the voices and that feeling like people were watching me or marking me somehow… that’s been around for maybe a year and a half now.\n\nSome days it’s not as bad, so I try not to make a big thing of it, but it hasn’t really gone away. Is that the kind of time frame you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, a lot more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI used to do more with people. I liked taking pictures, playing chess, going to the mosque, even cooking extra food for neighbors sometimes. I wasn’t some social butterfly, but I had things I cared about.\n\nNow… the camera just stays in the drawer. And when people call, I don’t always answer. I tell myself I’m just tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel like being around anybody much.\n\nI don’t mean to sound bad. It’s just… yeah, I’m less interested than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not feeling especially up or especially depressed, do you still find that your interest in people and activities is much lower than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not really high or really down, it’s still there.\n\nI mean, I try to tell myself I’m just getting older or tired from work, but it’s different than before. I used to like taking pictures, playing chess, going to the mosque, cooking extra for neighbors… normal things like that.\n\nNow the camera just sits in the drawer, and if people call, a lot of times I don’t answer. I don’t want to seem rude or ungrateful. I just… don’t feel the pull to do those things like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems overall—the voices, the feeling people were signaling about you, and the changes in your thinking—been going on for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s been more than six months.\n\nI guess if I’m being honest, it started after that brief detention, you know, the misunderstanding at the building. Since then it’s been… about two years and five months, something like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but overall, yeah, it’s been going on that long. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least one week when you felt unusually energized or powerful most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m bragging or anything, but there have been times where I felt really high, like… powerful, like I could handle anything.\n\nIt would go on for days, most of the day. I’d be laughing a lot, talking loud with everybody, feeling like people should listen to me. And usually I’d think I was just in a good mood, you know?\n\nBut if somebody questioned me or acted like I was wrong, I could snap. Like shout over something small. I’m not proud of that. But yes… I think there have been times like that, at least a week. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Because of these problems, have you become unable to keep up with your work or basic self-care compared with how you were before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still try to manage, doctor. I don’t want it to sound like I just gave up.\n\nBut compared to before, yes. I’ve missed shifts, and I’ve lost some cleaning work because people said they couldn’t rely on me, or they didn’t understand what I was saying sometimes. That was hard to hear.\n\nAnd outside work, I stopped going to the mosque as much, and I don’t really go to family gatherings like I used to. People invite me, but… I don’t always show up, or I leave things messy and unfinished.\n\nThere have been a couple times where it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help. I’m not proud of that. I’m just trying to answer you honestly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was because of the high energy, exactly. I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nThere were times people got worried and emergency help got involved, but whether that counts as being hospitalized for that high period… I’m not sure. I don’t remember it that neatly, like, “this was for that.” Sorry, doctor. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: At any time, have the voice and the feeling that people were signaling about you happened only when you were in one of those high-energy or depressed periods?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was only during those times.\n\nI mean, I wish I could say it was just when I was really high or really down, because that would make more sense, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I just gave up or anything, but there have been stretches where most days I’d wake up and just feel… heavy.\n\nLike after morning prayer, I’d sit on the edge of the bed and just stay there a while. Empty, I guess. Like there wasn’t much point in cleaning myself up or going outside.\n\nIt’s hard to say it the right way, but yes… it has lasted longer than two weeks before.\n\nDoctor: Across the whole time you’ve been dealing with the voices and people signaling about you, have you been in either a clear manic state or a depressed state for most of that time?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I think most of the time it’s been when I’m more keyed up.\n\nI don’t mean out of control, exactly, but I get going and I talk too much, and I start making these big cleaning plans that are probably more than I can really do. It feels like I have to keep moving, like I can’t slow myself down.\n\nSo… yes, if I’m answering it right, I’d say most of the time since all this started, I’ve been in those stretches more than the low ones.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms begin after you had a medical illness, head injury, seizure, or started using any drugs or new medicines?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… not that I can point to.\n\nI mean, I haven’t had some big head injury or anything like that that I remember. And I’m not aware of seizures. As far as drugs or new medicines, I don’t think it started from anything like that.\n\nIf there’s a certain kind of illness or medicine you mean, you can ask me straight, but… I don’t think that’s how it began.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling people were signaling about you while you were not manic and not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there were times like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were some calmer weeks where I wasn’t really up like that, and I wasn’t down in that heavy way either. I was just kind of… normal enough, I guess.\n\nBut I still heard the man in the vents during that time. And I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. That went on more than two weeks, I’m pretty sure.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and feeling that people were signaling about you come on very suddenly, within about two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor.\n\nIt didn’t feel like one day I was fine and then all of a sudden everything started within a week or two. But I also wasn’t keeping track that carefully, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nI guess it felt more like it kind of built up, but… if there’s a specific way you want me to think about it, I can try.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling that people were signaling about you lasted for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s been more than a month.\n\nI don’t like saying it like that, but the voices and that feeling like people were watching me or marking me somehow… that’s been around for maybe a year and a half now.\n\nSome days it’s not as bad, so I try not to make a big thing of it, but it hasn’t really gone away. Is that the kind of time frame you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, a lot more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI used to do more with people. I liked taking pictures, playing chess, going to the mosque, even cooking extra food for neighbors sometimes. I wasn’t some social butterfly, but I had things I cared about.\n\nNow… the camera just stays in the drawer. And when people call, I don’t always answer. I tell myself I’m just tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel like being around anybody much.\n\nI don’t mean to sound bad. It’s just… yeah, I’m less interested than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not feeling especially up or especially depressed, do you still find that your interest in people and activities is much lower than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not really high or really down, it’s still there.\n\nI mean, I try to tell myself I’m just getting older or tired from work, but it’s different than before. I used to like taking pictures, playing chess, going to the mosque, cooking extra for neighbors… normal things like that.\n\nNow the camera just sits in the drawer, and if people call, a lot of times I don’t answer. I don’t want to seem rude or ungrateful. I just… don’t feel the pull to do those things like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems overall—the voices, the feeling people were signaling about you, and the changes in your thinking—been going on for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s been more than six months.\n\nI guess if I’m being honest, it started after that brief detention, you know, the misunderstanding at the building. Since then it’s been… about two years and five months, something like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but overall, yeah, it’s been going on that long. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least one week when you felt unusually energized or powerful most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m bragging or anything, but there have been times where I felt really high, like… powerful, like I could handle anything.\n\nIt would go on for days, most of the day. I’d be laughing a lot, talking loud with everybody, feeling like people should listen to me. And usually I’d think I was just in a good mood, you know?\n\nBut if somebody questioned me or acted like I was wrong, I could snap. Like shout over something small. I’m not proud of that. But yes… I think there have been times like that, at least a week. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Because of these problems, have you become unable to keep up with your work or basic self-care compared with how you were before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still try to manage, doctor. I don’t want it to sound like I just gave up.\n\nBut compared to before, yes. I’ve missed shifts, and I’ve lost some cleaning work because people said they couldn’t rely on me, or they didn’t understand what I was saying sometimes. That was hard to hear.\n\nAnd outside work, I stopped going to the mosque as much, and I don’t really go to family gatherings like I used to. People invite me, but… I don’t always show up, or I leave things messy and unfinished.\n\nThere have been a couple times where it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help. I’m not proud of that. I’m just trying to answer you honestly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was because of the high energy, exactly. I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nThere were times people got worried and emergency help got involved, but whether that counts as being hospitalized for that high period… I’m not sure. I don’t remember it that neatly, like, “this was for that.” Sorry, doctor. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: At any time, have the voice and the feeling that people were signaling about you happened only when you were in one of those high-energy or depressed periods?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was only during those times.\n\nI mean, I wish I could say it was just when I was really high or really down, because that would make more sense, maybe. But there were calmer weeks too, when I wasn’t especially energized and I wasn’t deeply depressed.\n\nAnd even then… for more than a couple weeks, I still heard the man in the vents, and I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. I know that sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you heard the voice or felt people were signaling about you even though you were not feeling unusually high and not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there have been times like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were calmer weeks where I wasn’t really up or down like that. I was just more normal, I guess. And still, for more than two weeks, I could hear the man in the vents, and I still thought people in the building were signaling about me.\n\nThat’s what you’re asking, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I wasn’t some social butterfly, but I had things I cared about.\n\nNow… the camera just stays in the drawer. And when people call, I don’t always answer. I tell myself I’m just tired or busy, but really I just don’t feel like being around anybody much.\n\nI don’t mean to sound bad. It’s just… yeah, I’m less interested than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not feeling especially up or especially depressed, do you still find that your interest in people and activities is much lower than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not really high or really down, it’s still there.\n\nI mean, I try to tell myself I’m just getting older or tired from work, but it’s different than before. I used to like taking pictures, playing chess, going to the mosque, cooking extra for neighbors… normal things like that.\n\nNow the camera just sits in the drawer, and if people call, a lot of times I don’t answer. I don’t want to seem rude or ungrateful. I just… don’t feel the pull to do those things like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems overall—the voices, the feeling people were signaling about you, and the changes in your thinking—been going on for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s been more than six months.\n\nI guess if I’m being honest, it started after that brief detention, you know, the misunderstanding at the building. Since then it’s been… about two years and five months, something like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but overall, yeah, it’s been going on that long. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least one week when you felt unusually energized or powerful most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m bragging or anything, but there have been times where I felt really high, like… powerful, like I could handle anything.\n\nIt would go on for days, most of the day. I’d be laughing a lot, talking loud with everybody, feeling like people should listen to me. And usually I’d think I was just in a good mood, you know?\n\nBut if somebody questioned me or acted like I was wrong, I could snap. Like shout over something small. I’m not proud of that. But yes… I think there have been times like that, at least a week. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Because of these problems, have you become unable to keep up with your work or basic self-care compared with how you were before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still try to manage, doctor. I don’t want it to sound like I just gave up.\n\nBut compared to before, yes. I’ve missed shifts, and I’ve lost some cleaning work because people said they couldn’t rely on me, or they didn’t understand what I was saying sometimes. That was hard to hear.\n\nAnd outside work, I stopped going to the mosque as much, and I don’t really go to family gatherings like I used to. People invite me, but… I don’t always show up, or I leave things messy and unfinished.\n\nThere have been a couple times where it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help. I’m not proud of that. I’m just trying to answer you honestly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During one of those high-energy periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was because of the high energy, exactly. I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nThere were times people got worried and emergency help got involved, but whether that counts as being hospitalized for that high period… I’m not sure. I don’t remember it that neatly, like, “this was for that.” Sorry, doctor. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: At any time, have the voice and the feeling that people were signaling about you happened only when you were in one of those high-energy or depressed periods?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was only during those times.\n\nI mean, I wish I could say it was just when I was really high or really down, because that would make more sense, maybe. But there were calmer weeks too, when I wasn’t especially energized and I wasn’t deeply depressed.\n\nAnd even then… for more than a couple weeks, I still heard the man in the vents, and I still felt like people in the building were signaling about me. I know that sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you heard the voice or felt people were signaling about you even though you were not feeling unusually high and not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there have been times like that.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were calmer weeks where I wasn’t really up or down like that. I was just more normal, I guess. And still, for more than two weeks, I could hear the man in the vents, and I still thought people in the building were signaling about me.\n\nThat’s what you’re asking, right?\n\nDoctor: During a time when you were not feeling unusually high and not feeling depressed, did the voice and the feeling that people were signaling about you continue for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, yes.\n\nThere were some calmer weeks where I wasn’t really high or depressed, at least not like the other times. And it still kept going for more than two weeks—the man in the vents, and feeling like people in the building were signaling about me.\n\nI know that sounds bad, but I’m trying to answer it honestly."
